Reference counts are direct Markdown links to a lexicon-term anchor in the current `publish/` materials, counted on 1 October 2026. “Lexicon entries” counts word-or-phrase rows rather than individual aliases. “Term entries found” counts entries with at least one current Student Guide match; “linked entries” counts entries with at least one current Educator Guide link.

This lexicon lists Bitcoin and Bitcoin-adjacent terms that a beginner may need explained. It is the shared terminology authority for the Student Guide, Educator Guide, and Diploma activities. Each entry has a stable anchor for links from those materials.

Link a term only when it is used in a Bitcoin, economic, or computing sense and the linked definition explains that specific use. Leave ordinary English uses and incidental word matches unlinked; use the more specific entry when one exists, such as Lightning liquidity for channel capacity.

For **privacy**, link only uses where the word is a noun in its own right (for example, `protect privacy` or `financial privacy`). Do not link attributive uses that modify another noun, including shared modifiers in lists (for example, `privacy risks` or `privacy and safety boundaries`).

Student Guide line links are a snapshot of the current [Student Guide](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md). Each matching line number opens its containing section, so a term with multiple matches appears more than once. Longer listed phrases take precedence over terms within them. Only terms with at least one matching line are listed.

The standalone Educator Guide reference table at the end lists each linked-use line in body text and tables, linking its line number to the containing section. Only terms with at least one linked-use line are listed. Diploma activities use the same anchors where a term needs definition; activity-linked terms are validated against this lexicon. Headings, existing links, and ordinary uses with a different meaning are not relinked.

Guide traceability was refreshed on 1 October 2026 from the published guides. Run `python3 tools/refresh_lexicon.py --check` after guide edits to audit the snapshot and linked-use metadata.

| [403](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md#beginner-monetary-history-from-barter-to-digital-money) |  | <a id="term-issuer"></a>**Issuer** | The authority, institution, or system that creates and puts money or claims into circulation. |

| [405](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md#beginner-monetary-history-from-barter-to-digital-money) |  | <a id="term-debasement"></a>**Debasement** | Reducing a coin’s valuable-metal content while keeping its stated value the same. |
| [405](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md#beginner-monetary-history-from-barter-to-digital-money) |  | <a id="term-value"></a>**Value** | The usefulness or importance of something to a person. |

| [407](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md#beginner-monetary-history-from-barter-to-digital-money) |  | <a id="term-paper-claim"></a>**Paper claim** | A paper record representing a claim on an issuer for a stated asset or amount. |

| [599](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md#key-takeaways) |  | <a id="term-purchasing-power"></a>**Purchasing power** | The amount of goods and services a unit of money can buy. |

| [599](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-sg-26.10.md#key-takeaways) |  | <a id="term-privacy"></a>**Privacy** | The ability to limit who can collect, see, use, or share information about you, such as your name, contact details, location, and financial activity. When using Bitcoin, this includes limiting how your identity is linked to addresses, balances, and payments. Bitcoin’s public transaction history means privacy is not automatic, and privacy does not mean that all activity is invisible. |

| [3439](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Encryption**](#term-encryption) | Transforming information so only an authorized party can read it. |
| [3439](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Digital signature**](#term-digital-signature) | A mathematical proof created with a private key that authorizes a message or transaction. |
| [3440](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Proof of work**](#term-proof-of-work) | The process miners use to compete to propose blocks by performing costly computation. |
| [3440](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Miner**](#term-miner) | A participant that builds candidate blocks and performs proof-of-work computation. |
| [3440](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Node**](#term-node) | Software or a computer running Bitcoin software and checking the network’s rules. |
| [3440](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Candidate block**](#term-candidate-block) | A proposed block that has not yet been accepted into the chain. |
| [3443](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Price**](#term-price) | The amount exchanged for a good, service, or asset. |
| [3444](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Sound money**](#term-sound-money) | A judgment for money whose supply is difficult to expand and whose properties support reliable use over time. |
| [3445](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Ledger**](#term-ledger) | A record of transactions; Bitcoin’s ledger is copied and checked across many nodes. |
| [3446](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Private key**](#term-private-key) | Secret information that lets a wallet authorize spending; anyone who obtains it may control the funds. |
| [3447](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Seed phrase / recovery phrase**](#term-seed-phrase) | An ordered list of secret words that many wallets use to restore keys. |
| [3450](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Custody**](#term-custody) | Control of Bitcoin keys. |
| [3451](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Node**](#term-node) | Software or a computer running Bitcoin software and checking the network’s rules. |
| [3452](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Confirmation**](#term-confirmation) | A count showing how many valid blocks include or follow the block containing a transaction; more confirmations make its place in the blockchain more costly to rewrite. |
| [3453](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**Privacy**](#term-privacy) | The ability to limit who can collect, see, use, or share information about you, such as your name, contact details, location, and financial activity. When using Bitcoin, this includes limiting how your identity is linked to addresses, balances, and payments. Bitcoin’s public transaction history means privacy is not automatic, and privacy does not mean that all activity is invisible. |
| [3454](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**KYC**](#term-kyc) | Identity checks required by a financial service. |
| [3454](mfb-bd-26.10/mfb-bd-eg-26.10.md#17-quick-terminology-and-misconception-sensitive-distinctions) |  | [**No-KYC** (preferred; also called **non-KYC**)](#term-no-kyc) | A path collecting less or no standard KYC information.
